# Feature review ledger

Generated by `node tooling/scripts/review-ledger.mjs render`. Edit `review-index.json`, then regenerate.

Inventory: 805 source/capability/entrypoint groups across 62 semantic review questions. Observation: 2026-09-15.

Review queue: 39 pending reviews across 39 pending questions; 54 reviews in total. Only the configured core architecture questions share a review. Other features remain independent.

Local working-tree snapshot; base commit: f3019bcc10751cdcf6a8ebf8562d4e658e7ba07d. Source fingerprint: 5d39fe304fb3172304bc4e288d0886e9998e036afd95908f3a066db7845b88ae. Current inventory observation: matching. This includes local files and does not claim that they are on GitHub.

Bounded coverage: 3673 distinct files, 84 declared PLUGINS names, 42 actual Plite example catalog keys. Source groups are census entries, not independently reviewed features. Membership can overlap; totals do not add to a count of user capabilities.

Boundary: Editable package source/exports and packaging/proof census; copied registry and values; actual shared Plite example catalog/loaders/implementations; editor browser specs and runners; AI service routes; editor benchmark and tooling inputs. Site navigation/marketing, generated public output, templates, dependencies, caches and historical artifact corpora are excluded. Each question has explicit owner/consumer/proof inputs; directory membership alone does not establish semantic or behavior coverage.

Scores estimate review payoff (0–10), not architecture quality. Only justified review prerequisites constrain order; related questions supply context. Payoff ranks eligible scopes, with AI last. A directly requested feature still takes precedence. Product implementation stays in its owning plan.

Source matching does not certify behavior. Review, adoption, proof and inspection depth remain independent. Each question names actual owners, consumers, selected proof inputs and remaining gaps; a file assigned to a census group is not a completed architecture assessment. Draft evidence includes those declared inputs; queue prerequisites never silently expand its fingerprints.

Groups are one investigation with a verdict for every member question. Review completion counts recorded assessments; source freshness, adoption and proof remain visible under each question.

<a id="plite-core"></a>
## Plite core

Review document/runtime ownership, typed extension composition and command authority together.

Run `$best-api-review audit plite-core`. Use `node tooling/scripts/review-ledger.mjs lookup plite-core` for every member's owners, consumers, proof and history. Draft and record each member by its stable question ID.

- [Runtime, document roots and view lifetime](#runtime) (`runtime`): Which state belongs to one document, shared root or mounted view, and can competing lifetime owners be removed?
- [Document state, fields and effects](#state) (`state`): Which values must persist with a document and which belong to transient view or application state?
- [Extension installation and composition](#schema) (`schema`): How should typed extensions contribute and reconfigure schema and behavior without duplicate configuration authorities?
- [Commands, availability and shortcuts](#commands) (`commands`): What is the smallest command contract that shares availability and intent between headless calls, shortcuts and UI controls?

<a id="plite-view"></a>
## Plite view architecture

Review mounted view lifetime, selection, browser input, focus and geometry as one architecture.

Run `$best-api-review audit plite-view`. Use `node tooling/scripts/review-ledger.mjs lookup plite-view` for every member's owners, consumers, proof and history. Draft and record each member by its stable question ID.

- [React integration and rendering](#react) (`react`): What is the smallest React integration for canonical editor state, rendering and component lifetime?
- [Selections, locations and rebasing](#selection) (`selection`): Which location guarantees survive edits, deletion, movement and root changes, and where do text, node and range targets need distinct contracts?
- [Native input and DOM reconciliation](#native) (`native`): Which owner should reconcile browser input, IME, DOM mutations and document changes without competing write paths?
- [Focus, accessible navigation and read-only interaction](#accessibility) (`accessibility`): Which focus and navigation guarantees must hold for voids, owned controls, inactive editors and read-only views?
- [Widgets, floating controls and geometry](#geometry) (`geometry`): Which mounted view owns widget and selection geometry, and can duplicate position stores or observers disappear?

<a id="plate-core"></a>
## Plate core

Review the Plate plugin/facade contract together with its public package boundaries.

Run `$best-api-review audit plate-core`. Use `node tooling/scripts/review-ledger.mjs lookup plate-core` for every member's owners, consumers, proof and history. Draft and record each member by its stable question ID.

- [Plate plugin API and Plite facade](#plate-api) (`plate-api`): Which Plate defaults and public plugin contracts serve a product job, and which duplicate an adequate Plite owner?
- [Public exports and package boundaries](#distribution) (`distribution`): Which independently consumed entrypoints justify their distribution boundary, and which facades or optional-dependency boundaries can disappear?

<a id="model"></a>
## Document structure and identity

What is the smallest schema-valid document and identity contract that all editors and codecs must preserve?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: adopted. Proof: partial. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-11-model-document-boundary / stale.

Payoff 10/10: Document shape and stable identity constrain every operation, persisted value and feature.

Review prerequisites: none. Document identity and validity are the starting laws for this queue. Next owner: plite-plan.

Related questions/history (not prerequisites): [transactions](#transactions), [reads](#reads), [runtime](#runtime), [schema](#schema).

Owners: [schema-definition.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/schema-definition.ts), [document-shape.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/document-shape.ts), [initial-value.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/initial-value.ts), [element.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/interfaces/element.ts), [editor-schema.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/editor-schema.ts), [schema-compiler.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/schema-compiler.ts), [editor.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/interfaces/editor.ts), [node-keys.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/utils/node-keys.ts).

Consumers: [editor](../../packages/platejs/src/lib/editor), [create-editor.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/create-editor.ts), [public-state.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/public-state.ts), [compiled-slice-fitter.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/slice-fit/compiled-slice-fitter.ts), [compilePlateModel.ts](../../packages/platejs/src/internal/plugin/compilePlateModel.ts), [markdownConversion.ts](../../packages/platejs/src/markdown/lib/internal/markdownConversion.ts), [ElementIdPlugin.ts](../../packages/platejs/src/lib/plugins/element-id/ElementIdPlugin.ts), [richtext.tsx](../../apps/www/src/app/(app)/examples/plite/_examples/richtext.tsx), [multi-root-document.tsx](../../apps/www/src/app/(app)/examples/plite/_examples/multi-root-document.tsx).

Proof entry points (not replayed by this ledger): [schema-identity-contract.test.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/schema-identity-contract.test.ts), [schema-definition.test.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/schema-definition.test.ts), [schema-compiler-laws.test.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/schema-compiler-laws.test.ts), [node-key-view-contract.test.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/node-key-view-contract.test.ts), [value-codec.test.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/value-codec.test.ts), [document-boundary-probe.mjs](../../docs/plans/artifacts/model-api-review/document-boundary-probe.mjs).

Additional evidence inputs: [package.json](../../packages/plitejs/package.json), [tsconfig.json](../../packages/plitejs/tsconfig.json), [plite-source-aliases.ts](../../config/plite-source-aliases.ts), [plite-source-test-setup.ts](../../config/plite-source-test-setup.ts), [plite-test-jsx.js](../../config/plite-test-jsx.js), [workspace-source-entries.mjs](../../config/workspace-source-entries.mjs), [bunTestSetup.ts](../../tooling/config/bunTestSetup.ts), [plite.md](../../docs/vision/plite.md), [schema-and-identity.md](../../.agents/skills/best-api/rules/schema-and-identity.md), [schema.mdx](../../content/docs/(guides)/schema.mdx).

Inspection: Model types, construction, compiled schema identity, document assertion/fitting/replacement, runtime node keys, Plate lowering, persisted IDs and raw/codec consumers inspected. The malformed-container probe ran red; 33 focused and adjacent tests, the core source-first typecheck and scoped lint pass after the shared validator repair, including primary-root redefinition.

Gaps: The selected document-boundary repair has focused model proof. Full model/runtime/native/performance closure and unrelated concurrent aggregate-check failures remain outside this adoption claim.

Plans/context: [best-api-review.md](../../docs/analysis/best-api-review.md), [2026-09-11-model-document-boundary-patch.md](../../docs/plans/2026-09-11-model-document-boundary-patch.md)

History:

- [2026-07-23: The July assessment retained read/update ownership while identifying extension identity and host-boundary debt.](review-records/2026-07-23-api-model.json) — historical; no recovered verdict; initial. This is a July source assessment, not a current score or completed implementation. Renamed public owners and later doctrine require reconciliation on the next review.

- [2026-09-11: Pursue one private Plite document-shape validation owner across construction, schema assertion and replacement; retain the JSON tree and separate runtime, persisted and schema identities.](review-records/2026-09-11-model-document-boundary.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. 46 existing tests passed in five source-first package files. A separate 16-row, four-operation-per-row public-API probe reproduced 12 malformed-container cases in raw and closed-schema editors: assertDocument accepts, construction rejects, direct and persisted replacement accept and discard the malformed field. Valid-document and invalid-node controls behave consistently. No fix, browser/device proof, full static typecheck, packed artifact validation, performance gain or exhaustive model audit is claimed. No new runtime store/index/traversal target is selected; traversal/representation changes would require the owning scale probe.

<a id="reads"></a>
## Reads, snapshots and subscriptions

Which canonical read and snapshot owner can replace repeated traversals or duplicate subscriptions without changing observable state?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: not-assessed. Proof: partial. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-11-reads-demand-driven-invalidation / stale.

Payoff 10/10: Repeated reads and broad subscriptions multiply across large documents and many mounted consumers.

Review prerequisites: [model](#model). Snapshots and subscriptions must observe the canonical document value and identity consistently. Next owner: plite-plan.

Related questions/history (not prerequisites): [transactions](#transactions), [react](#react), [performance](#performance).

Owners: [editor-reads.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/editor-reads.ts), [editor-read-runtime.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/editor-read-runtime.ts), [snapshot-index.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/snapshot-index.ts), [public-state.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/public-state.ts), [commit.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/commit.ts), [listener-state.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/listener-state.ts), [use-editor-selector.tsx](../../packages/plitejs/src/react/hooks/use-editor-selector.tsx).

At 8,192 paragraphs the isolated cached prototype cuts first-query index calls from 32,788 to two and retains zero repeated-read index calls. Preserve live read topology, historical snapshots, middleware and DOM-owned rendering distinctions.](review-records/2026-09-11-reads-demand-driven-invalidation.json) — review; pursue; initial. 56 current-source tests pass: 19 read-view/node-key, 10 middleware, 21 commit metadata and six React selectors. The prototype tests warm materialized primary-root indexes at 32/1,024/8,192 paragraphs with 0/1/32/all watchers, five alternating samples, 70 guard-key observations and exact parity. The uncached prototype is preserved and rejected for repeated-read cost. The cached isolated membership law passes, but complete React dispatch remains provisional: cold/lazy index behavior, multiple roots, all mutation kinds, retained memory, DOM bindings and native ordering need owning adoption proof. Browser specs were inspected, not run. No end-to-end latency, full scope coverage, product implementation or release claim.

<a id="transactions"></a>
## Atomic updates and document changes

What becomes observable when updates commit, nest, fail or repair content, and which overlapping mutation authorities can disappear?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: adopted. Proof: partial. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-11-transactions-synchronous-boundary / stale.

Payoff 10/10: All editing, history and asynchronous consumers depend on one coherent commit/failure law.

Review prerequisites: [model](#model). Transaction atomicity and repair must preserve canonical document validity and identity. Next owner: plite-plan.

Related questions/history (not prerequisites): [model](#model), [history](#history), [authored](#authored).

Owners: [document-change.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/document-change.ts), [commit.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/commit.ts), [public-state.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/public-state.ts), [editor-lifecycle-api.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/editor-lifecycle-api.ts), [plugin.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/plugin.ts), [command-registry.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/command-registry.ts), [editor-runtime-view.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/editor-runtime-view.ts).

Inspection: Bounded synchronous-author repair locally adopted in Plite transaction guards, spec construction and named-root view forwarding. Final focused proof passes 187 tests across 10 files, including 31 regressions, plus 12 source-first typecheck tasks and scoped lint. Rollback, retired continuations, ordinary returns, read promises, callable/nested methods and prepared-spec composition are covered by matching local fingerprints; the original review record remains historical.

Gaps: The selected thenable-author repair has focused package/API proof. Whole-transaction architecture, browser/native parity, full-checkout closure, publication and performance remain outside this adoption claim. Existing update, prepared-spec and canonical-change owners are retained; no public API or durable-law change is selected.

- [2026-09-11: Pursue consistent rejection of thenable transaction authors at existing Plite extension-write and pure-spec boundaries. Keep immediate updates, prepared command specs and canonical document changes; no broader transaction rewrite is justified by the current jobs.](review-records/2026-09-11-transactions-synchronous-boundary.json) — review; pursue; initial. 94 existing cases pass across five package files. A source-bound 14-row public probe finds nine accepted thenable-author paths: seven publish, two return a prepared spec. Three delayed continuations show a normal async update rolling back while an extension method and command builder retain their first write after the later write fails. All 41 file inputs, one consumer-directory fingerprint and 11 scope-group fingerprints are unchanged across the final proof. This is a bounded authority review, not an exhaustive mutation-algorithm audit. No product fix, type-level rejection, new guard runtime, performance improvement, browser/native parity or strict checkout closure is certified. The selected next owner is Patch for the reproduced behavior, not an accepted new runtime architecture.

<a id="large-documents"></a>
## Large documents and partial DOM coverage

Which costs require fewer mounted nodes, and what selection, find and export guarantees survive partial DOM coverage?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: in-progress. Proof: partial. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-12-large-documents-deep-contract / stale.

Payoff 10/10: Large documents test rendering and read costs independently of whether the product has pages.

Review prerequisites: [reads](#reads), [runtime](#runtime). Canonical reads and mounted-view ownership determine which costs partial DOM coverage can remove. Next owner: plite-plan.

Related questions/history (not prerequisites): [native](#native), [geometry](#geometry), [pagination](#pagination), [exports](#exports), [performance](#performance).

Inspection: Deep reassessment covers the original eight groups and direct pagination/clipboard/lifetime/package dependencies. Reconciled 126 bounded rows and 83 adoption paths; five current-contract counterexamples, three page projection comparisons, runtime dependency build, nine SSR cases and proposed callers checked. Prefer one omission permission, stable active DOM and pagination-owned canvas rendering; withdraw coordinate normalization and new CSS marker.

Gaps: Production runtime not accepted. P0-P4 require stable host and active-descendant identity, composed clipboard policies, committed materialization, direct page geometry, initial-window/hydration and packed dependency/application bundle proof. Projection-only counts do not prove native rendering or total page cost. No native Find UI, AT, print, IME/device or collaboration claim; next is the P1/P2 renderer prototype under Plite Plan with Benchmark.

Plans/context: [2026-09-11-large-documents-api-review.md](../../docs/plans/2026-09-11-large-documents-api-review.md)

History:

- [2026-09-11: Pursue an explicit DOM omission contract: auto silently mounts 32 of 1001 blocks despite the DOM-present Vision default. Challenge segment/staged duplication and inert findPolicy; retain the shared coverage bridge. Runtime consolidation remains provisional.](review-records/2026-09-11-large-documents-explicit-dom-coverage.json) — review; pursue; initial. Eight expected inventory groups reviewed, none excluded, no unresolved group dispositions. Five existing owning Vitest suites pass 112 contracts with React Compiler transformation. Lexical census covers 2142 source files and finds only findPolicy declarations/forwarding/writers. Post-run source capture is not an atomic whole-checkout release attestation. No fresh matched runtime scale, browser/native Find UI, accessibility, print, IME/mobile or collaboration proof. Renderer consolidation is provisional; adoption/proof states remain unchanged.

- [2026-09-11: Specify virtualize on the existing Editable and privately normalize pagination layout; remove strategy taxonomy, automatic segments, staged retention and public metrics/offset protocols. Defer runtime, geometry and packaging acceptance: source drift and incomplete probe coverage prevent a passing comparison.](review-records/2026-09-11-large-documents-rendering-design.json) — review; defer; defers. Three same-thread candidates; no independent workers or cross-model judge. 111 bounded rows reviewed: 50 delete, 6 localize, 28 retain, 27 runtime-gated; no missing dedicated declarations, unrelated nested editor algorithms excluded from the deletion audit. Proposed raw/Plate callers compile. The 100-block production browser packet completed but captured authored inputs changed; it is diagnostic only. The 1001 attempt was interrupted after preflight drift; stress was not run. Frozen end-position coverage exceeds the runner start/middle coverage. No consolidated implementation, current-source speed, native Find UI, accessibility, print, IME/device, collaboration or packed dependency acceptance.

- [2026-09-12: Prefer one explicit omission permission and stable active DOM; delete pagination’s second generic viewport/coordinate owner and keep its existing canvas. Withdraw public fragment normalization and the new root marker. Source-stable probes expose host/child replacement, implicit paginated unit filtering, mixed copy-policy failure, static dependency reachability and SSR omission. Runtime adoption remains gated.](review-records/2026-09-12-large-documents-deep-contract.json) — review; defer; supersedes. Five current-contract package probe cases and three projection cases pass. JSDOM proves host/key/clipboard behavior only; pagination used injected 400px rectangles and the fixture retained all DOM children. Page comparison proves root-key/canvas-extent parity and property-read counts at 100/1000/10000 supplied page items, not total layout cost, speed or native geometry. Actual runtime build kept 475 inputs stable; Node SSR covered nine cases, with no window/document. Proposed calls typecheck only as declarations. No production target, hydration, native Find UI, AT, print, real IME/device, collaboration or packed clean-install/application-bundle acceptance. Sequential same-thread comparison; no independent workers or model-family review. P0-P4 and S1-S7 are planned, not implemented.

<a id="selection"></a>
## Selections, locations and rebasing

Which location guarantees survive edits, deletion, movement and root changes, and where do text, node and range targets need distinct contracts?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: not-assessed. Proof: not-replayed. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-12-selection-distinct-lifetimes / stale.

Payoff 10/10: Selection and rebasing preserve user intent across clipboard, history, tables and annotations.

Review prerequisites: [transactions](#transactions). Locations must preserve document identity through the established transaction behavior. Next owner: none (Stop).

Related questions/history (not prerequisites): [annotations](#annotations), [native](#native), [accessibility](#accessibility), [table](#table).

Owners: [selection-protocol.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/selection-protocol.ts), [selection-state.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/selection-state.ts), [anchor.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/anchor.ts), [location.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/interfaces/location.ts), [selection.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/interfaces/selection.ts), [view-selection.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/react/view-selection.ts), [projected-selection-target.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/react/editable/projected-selection-target.ts).

Consumers: [runtime-selection-engine.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/react/editable/runtime-selection-engine.ts), [inlines.tsx](../../apps/www/src/app/(app)/examples/plite/_examples/inlines.tsx), [selection.ts](../../packages/platejs/src/features/table/lib/internal/selection.ts), [BaseMediaPlugin.ts](../../packages/platejs/src/features/media/lib/BaseMediaPlugin.ts), [synced-blocks.tsx](../../apps/www/src/app/(app)/examples/plite/_examples/synced-blocks.tsx).

Proof entry points (not replayed by this ledger): [authored-anchor-contract.test.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/authored-anchor-contract.test.ts), [root-location-contract.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/root-location-contract.ts).

Additional evidence inputs: [package.json](../../packages/plitejs/package.json), [tsconfig.json](../../packages/plitejs/tsconfig.json), [editor-runtime-view.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/editor-runtime-view.ts), [public-state.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/public-state.ts), [2026-08-31-promote-view-scoped-selection-ownership.md](../../docs/plans/2026-08-31-promote-view-scoped-selection-ownership.md), [plite.md](../../docs/vision/plite.md), [SKILL.md](../../.agents/skills/best-api/SKILL.md), [SKILL.md](../../.agents/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md).

Inspection: Reviewed text and exact directional node selection, range projections, anchor lifetime/deletion/persistence, named-root mapping and internal projected view-selection targeting. Table selection and asynchronous media insertion supply distinct current consumers; existing anchor/root assertions inspected, not executed.

Gaps: Stop applies to replacing these selected ownership boundaries. Mapping complexity, authored changes, same-root sibling view behavior and device/browser parity have not been replayed or performance-certified in this source review.

Plans/context: No specific prior plan linked; search before review.

Current compiled decision: [plite-view-ownership.md](../../docs/research/decisions/plite-view-ownership.md).

<a id="authored"></a>
## Authored changes and selective reversal

Which native contract should retain authorship and reversible edits without requiring a particular review UI?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: adopted. Proof: verified. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-13-authored-direct-checkpoint-implementation / stale.

Payoff 10/10: Native authored semantics now open from a direct current-state checkpoint without operation reduction or pending replay; the accepted production path clears the frozen Bun and Node scale gate.

Review prerequisites: [selection](#selection). Retained authorship and reversal require committed-change and rebasing guarantees; review UI is contextual. Next owner: task.

Related questions/history (not prerequisites): [history](#history), [suggestions](#suggestions), [diff](#diff), [collaboration](#collaboration).

Owners: [index.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/authored/index.ts), [authored-runtime.ts](../../packages/plitejs/src/core/authored-runtime.ts).

Current plan reports S1–S3 in progress. Native authored exports coexist with the independent BaseSuggestionPlugin implementation; no whole-plan completion is inferred.

- [2026-09-12: Pursue redesigning authored persistence so current review state loads without full operation reduction and pending-edit replay. Keep native authored semantics and measured bulk wins. One directly validated live checkpoint plus a causal frontier and bounded suffix is the candidate family; compact live-state versus retained-content representation remains provisional pending Best API and its embedded Benchmark gate.](review-records/2026-09-12-authored-scalability.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. Pursue authorizes further design only. Neither a direct live checkpoint nor a retained-content replacement was prototyped, benchmarked or accepted in this review. The current headless matched repair proves Bun 6.22s to 2.54s and Node 7.24s to 2.44s for 10055 pending insertion operations; 337 authored/Yjs tests, types and lint belong to that existing implementation. ProseMirror marks (16.6/31.8ms) lack equivalent causal history; plain document comparison is separate. All-pending load is not fixed merely by archiving closed history. Complete-load, first-edit, decisions, save, memory, malformed checkpoint, dependency/conflict, stable-anchor and delayed-peer proof must gate the selected representation. No browser/native, general scalability, all-feature API audit, new performance gain or publication claim is made. Earlier failed convergence research and prior receive-budget/build limits remain in their original artifacts.

- [2026-09-12: Retain initialValue/read.value and the optional authored capability. Prefer directly validated current authored facts over accepted roots; keep unified retained content as the structural challenger. The retained independent-text decoder clears the cost gate at about 68/72 ms versus 2.51 s native and 5.19 MB versus 17.12 MB. Reject the tested 44.25 MB redundant mirror control. Defer production runtime acceptance because the subset lacks native/causal semantics and incremental decisions; Task owns the remaining coupled Plite format/runtime/retention/proof job.](review-records/2026-09-12-authored-loadable-state.json) — review; defer; defers. Cost gate passes only for a detached independent-plain-text insertion representation. Native current baseline and candidate differ semantically. Candidate guards cover exact native text/author/status, detachment, identity/offset conservation, detached insert/accept/reject, save/reload and ten malformed inputs; no native anchor API, causal sequence/collision admission, stale decisions, dependencies, deleted/structural/property/root content, history compensation, retained-memory/closed-history scale or delayed-peer convergence. Detached commands scan all content; decision timings select different independent IDs and are not matched ratios. Old-format conversion costs 2.71 s once. Original full tests do not certify changed current Yjs/UI inputs. No product/browser/native-device, universal scalability or ProseMirror superiority claim.

- [2026-09-13: Pursue is now implemented and proven. One versioned authored checkpoint owns current facts and exact projection state; checksum-bound retained operation bodies stay cold. Registered codecs decode before the generic initial-metadata detachment pass, so safety no longer deep-copies the checkpoint twice. Public calls remain unchanged, load-time operation reduction and pending replay are deleted from the current path, and the Plate suggestion UI consumes the native owner.](review-records/2026-09-13-authored-direct-checkpoint-implementation.json) — review; pursue; reaffirms. The performance result is a deterministic all-pending 10,000-paragraph load benchmark with three samples per runtime, so it supports medians and ranges rather than tail percentiles. Browser proof is focused on Chromium and the suggestion route; no cross-browser matrix, heap ceiling, or universal ProseMirror equivalence is claimed. Decision latency grows by less than the frozen 10 ms allowance, and Node save remains below the frozen 2x limit.

<a id="clipboard"></a>
## Clipboard interchange and content fitting

What does transfer preserve across schemas and editor instances, and can one content-fitting owner replace feature-specific paste repair?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: adopted. Proof: verified. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-13-clipboard-transfer-convergence / stale.

Payoff 10/10: Clipboard loss and mismatched fitting affect every structure and external format.

Review prerequisites: [selection](#selection). Transferred content must fit valid document changes and retain the selection intent established by rebasing. Next owner: best-api.

Inspection: Accepted clipboard transfer target implemented across Plite core/DOM/React/authored/Yjs and Plate media/table/static consumers. Complete slices retain reachable roots, fitting and grouped placement stay private, domCommands.insertData replaces the public contribution pipeline, projected/static export uses exact source slices, and uploads start after committed placeholder insertion. Focused package, source-type, slow table, Chromium and direct cross-browser clipboard/drag proof passes; docs, doctrine, barrels, registries and changesets are reconciled. The canonical quiet-host production receipt has zero correctness or issue-budget failures: 10,000-line paste is 46.73 ms p50, full-selection copy is 17.06 ms p50, populated middle paste is 57.93 ms p50, and 50,000-block cut with copy is 28.33 ms p50.

Gaps: Aggregate Plite test typechecking and the managed browser matrix end in concurrent plugin/Suggestion source failures outside clipboard-owned partitions; the focused and direct cross-browser clipboard proof passes. Raw native-device clipboard behavior and publication are unclaimed.

## Collaborative synchronization and presence

Which native change and identity contracts should providers reuse for local, remote and reconnect behavior?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: adopted. Proof: verified. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-14-collaboration-lifetime-implementation / stale.

Payoff 9/10: Collaboration correctness constrains authored changes, history and document identity.

Review prerequisites: [selection](#selection). Local and remote changes must preserve transaction and selection identity laws before provider policy is compared. Next owner: task.

Related questions/history (not prerequisites): [persistence](#persistence), [history](#history), [runtime](#runtime), [authored](#authored).

Owners: [yjs](../../packages/plitejs/src/yjs).

Consumers: [yjs](../../packages/platejs/src/yjs), [yjs-collaboration.tsx](../../apps/www/src/app/(app)/examples/plite/_examples/yjs-collaboration.tsx), [yjs-hocuspocus.tsx](../../apps/www/src/app/(app)/examples/plite/_examples/yjs-hocuspocus.tsx).

Proof entry points (not replayed by this ledger): [admission-lifecycle-contract.spec.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/yjs/admission-lifecycle-contract.spec.ts), [YjsPlugin.api.spec.ts](../../packages/platejs/src/yjs/YjsPlugin.api.spec.ts), [yjs-collaboration-contracts.ts](../../packages/platejs/type-tests/yjs-collaboration-contracts.ts), [yjs-hocuspocus.test.ts](../../apps/plite/tests/plite-browser/donor/examples/yjs-hocuspocus.test.ts), [yjs-collaboration.mjs](../../benchmarks/slate-v2/donor/core/current/yjs-collaboration.mjs).

Additional evidence inputs: none declared.

Inspection: Implemented and rechecked all 12 collaboration census groups plus the shared editor-view lifetime owner. Apps own provider, readiness and seed authority; one native binding owns Yjs admission and identity; Plate adds a capability-inferred presentation facade. Pre-publication rejection, retained exactly-once retry, atomic readiness, controller/namespace/compaction ownership, active-view arbitration, exact exports and emitted types, generated registry consumers, a real Hocuspocus browser path and all seven balanced composite benchmark cohorts pass.

Gaps: No collaboration-owned design or proof gap remains. Manual one-hour and production soaks require an explicit soak request and are not part of this closure. Unrelated checkout-wide authored, suggestion, clipboard, media and table failures are recorded in the completed plan; no release or publication is claimed.

Task design plan owns the coupled contract, adoption and proof decisions; this review does not settle a final API.](review-records/2026-09-14-collaboration-lifetime-ownership.json) — review; pursue; initial. 69 existing Plite provider/awareness/schema/multi-root tests pass in four files; eight Plate adapter tests pass in one file. Two source-backed diagnostic cases reproduce external effects from discarded specs and aborted updates. Probe setup errors were corrected and documented. No real network/server, browser/native device, performance comparison, full CRDT/history soak or proposed implementation was executed. Census review is bounded API/architecture coverage, not exhaustive behavioral coverage. Inventory refresh updates observations only and does not refresh older reviews. The ownership cuts remain directionally sound, especially deleting transport commands, the duplicate Plate store and live read.yjs, but the plan was falsely labeled ready. It lacked a composite candidate; swallowed shared-effect failures, invalid waiting/error commit compensation, compaction replacement conflict, post-publication failure semantics and multi-view awareness arbitration remain unresolved. The review also cuts the unearned public Hocuspocus helper, retains YjsUpdatePolicy, makes API capabilities conditional, selects concrete demo seed ownership, adds an exact export/adoption matrix and rejects the order-confounded benchmark as acceptance.](review-records/2026-09-14-collaboration-plan-readiness.json) — review; defer; defers. No product implementation, TypeScript 7 packed declaration proof, real Hocuspocus server run, mounted browser proof or combined lifecycle/API candidate exists. Current shared-effect errors are swallowed, waiting/error writes use commit-plus-compensation, same-document replacement can conflict with compaction authority and post-publication observer failure cannot restore the old binding. The calibrated benchmark reports 15 passes but gives baseline the cold-first construction position twice, compares independent medians, misses a direct normal construction pair and measures only cursor-data API relocation. Claimed-empty required-minimum behavior was observed only in a disposable native probe. The corrected plan intentionally remains incomplete.

- [2026-09-14: Pursue and mark adopted. The collaboration lifetime redesign is implemented: apps own providers and readiness, the native binding owns admission and Yjs identity, Plate composes the binding without a second store, and view-bound presence lives under capability-inferred api.yjs. Provider commands, public lifecycle/controller internals, read.yjs and Yjs update/transaction APIs are deleted. Atomic readiness, retained retry, namespace/compaction ownership, exclusive seeding, exact-view arbitration, public declarations, real-provider browser behavior and all seven balanced performance cohorts pass.](review-records/2026-09-14-collaboration-lifetime-implementation.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. Manual one-hour and production soaks were not run because repository guidance requires an explicit soak request. No release or publication is claimed. Focused collaboration proof is green; broader checkout commands still stop on concurrent authored/suggestion API-key drift, clipboard internal-export bookkeeping, a Plate media caption split, a table type-instantiation error and an authored/clipboard browser insertion-offset mismatch. The plan records each failure and the independently passing affected stages.

Inspection: The retained-source implementation was inspected across its conditional public types, opaque lifetime owner, bounded package reader, exact eligibility checks, relationship-closed edited overlay, first-party provider and toolbar flow, current docs, generated registry, packed entrypoints, Chromium workflow, LibreOffice re-save, production benchmark, doctrine source and generated mirrors.

Gaps: The retained-source target is adopted and verified for exact unchanged review reuse plus safe closed root and one-section header/footer overlay. Body edits deliberately discard unknown body XML; multi-section reconstruction, arbitrary package preservation, Microsoft Word certification, external repository suites, persistent source reattachment and collaboration transport remain outside the claim. No publication is claimed.

No complete slow-suite, current packed-install, browser clipboard, native Word/LibreOffice, performance or arbitrary preservation proof. Source-revision manifest tracks fetched object reads independently of unchanged older checkout HEADs. Design, implementation and doctrine/doc repair remain outstanding.

Exact result/error/limit/comment types and HTML-versus-OOXML runtime remain for Task design and prototype.

- [2026-09-15: Pursue and mark adopted. WordPastePlugin remains a separate paste job. Public subpaths, callers, docs, doctrine, browser behavior, LibreOffice readability, package boundaries and production scale are verified.](review-records/2026-09-15-documents-canonical-conversion-implementation.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. LibreOffice proof establishes package readability, not Microsoft Word fidelity. No commit, push, PR, release or external publication is claimed.

- [2026-09-15: Pursue a Task design plan for explicit retained-source preservation. This supersedes only the prior blanket deferral of source retention: Plate already exposes import Word, edit and export Word as one first-party workflow, so diagnostics alone do not satisfy preservation of unrelated source content. Keep semantic conversion as the default and keep the verified import/export, projection, diagnostics, package-limit, paste and feature-owner boundaries. The retained source must be opt-in, opaque, source-bound and invalidation-aware; whole-ZIP passthrough, hidden editor state, a second DocxSession and an OOXML canonical-model replacement are rejected.](review-records/2026-09-15-documents-retained-source-reassessment.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. <a id="external-text"></a>
## External text editors and shared views

Which editing and selection authority may an external text view own while sharing canonical document text, history and other views?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: planned. Proof: partial. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-15-external-text-ordered-feedback / stale.

Payoff 9/10: External adapters have distinct focus, IME, selection and text-ownership contracts from native syntax rendering.

Review prerequisites: [runtime](#runtime), [selection](#selection). Shared-view lifetime and selection authority must be established before an external editor can own input. Next owner: task.

Related questions/history (not prerequisites): [code](#code), [native](#native), [history](#history), [authored](#authored).

Inspection: September 14 implementation adopts a concrete non-installable YjsPlugin factory with create, require and map. A private input-to-descriptor witness preserves native document, presence, compaction and validator-derived cursor capabilities through Plate adaptation and copied CollaborationPlugin composition. Mapping reuses the existing Plate author-stage input, compatibility and result owner; created descriptors retain extend and terminal configure. Handwritten Plate and copied forwarding factories and their overload/type mirrors are deleted. Focused package, copied UI, clean declaration, DCE, registry, doctrine and matched cost proof passes.

Gaps: The package-wide Plate typecheck and declaration bundle stop in the unrelated changed SuggestionPlugin.tsx on TS2883, TS4023 and TS7056; website API-reference checking consequently lacks platejs/dist/index.d.ts. The clean Yjs declaration partition passes after deleting stale incremental output. The benchmark baseline is reconstructed from the exact uncommitted local source captured before replacement. No browser matrix, package publication, release or external application migration is claimed.

History:

- [2026-09-12: Pursue contract design for one installed capability identity and lookup; retain Plate product semantics and leave replacement runtime unaccepted pending scale proof.](review-records/2026-09-12-plate-api-capability-identity.json) — review; pursue; initial. Observed 38 Base/plugin-access/codec-context tests, 18 React constructor/adapter tests and 20 package-proof runner fixture tests passed. No current packed-release, full typecheck, browser, native-device, performance or external-consumer proof. No replacement runtime is accepted. A paired current/target scale comparison remains required before adoption. Shared-checkout source may change after this recorded snapshot.

- [2026-09-13: Pursue restoring editor.plugin(Plugin) as the Plate product contract while retaining editor.extension(Extension) for raw extension access and the shared Plite installed owner. The September 12 plan explicitly rejected separate public portals because plugin projections include core capabilities; current source still has distinct descriptor domains, result types and a nominal dispatch into createPluginPortal. Inclusion of substrate capabilities does not eliminate the product consumer job. Preserve the registry/ancestry improvements and direct transaction groups; avoid a second installation system or two equivalent plugin spellings. Next owner: Task design plan for the exact descriptor boundary and adoption.](review-records/2026-09-13-plate-plugin-extension-boundary.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. Bounded source/plan/consumer/assertion review only. No tests, typechecks, browser or performance experiments were rerun. Existing production receipts measure identity/ancestry and transaction-map deletion, not the public spelling on an otherwise identical registry. Shared installation and direct transactions remain; proposed plugin access is unimplemented. Preserve historical implementation/proof state separately from this revised target. Future adoption must prove exact inference, raw adoption, optional lookup, nominal ancestry, candidate and view lifecycle, store/API identity and teaching parity. Any additional runtime machinery requires its own matched evidence.

- [2026-09-13: Pursue one neutral composable plugin descriptor shared by Plite and Plate, with typed Plate-owned declarations compiled against that same identity. This supersedes an isolated restoration of plugin/extension accessor names. Current Plate author stages, native/Plate capability branches, dependency remapping and creation of replacement defineExtension descriptors expose real deletion opportunities. Keep product compilation, schema semantics, runtime stores and view presentation in Plate; eliminate the second plugin engine and identity translation, not every product helper. A unified definePlugin/plugins/editor.plugin vocabulary becomes credible with that architecture. Runtime, type and adoption acceptance remain open; next owner is Task design plan shared plugin contract.](review-records/2026-09-13-shared-plugin-contract.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. Source, consumer and assertion inspection only. No new prototype, tests, typechecks, browser or benchmark execution. Existing contribution tests establish the current ordered contribution contract, not typed product composition feasibility. Earlier reference/transaction measurements do not accept this larger target. Before adoption, prove one raw capability, one configured headless plugin and one React plugin on the shared protocol, including prior-stage inference, schema, state/configuration isolation, nominal dependencies, candidate rollback, view binding and static/SSR isolation. Compare construction/configuration/runtime lookup/update costs, retained state and declaration/type costs with the current owner. Require actual adapter/descriptor deletion; a wrapper around both compilers fails. Naming is proposed, not implemented.

- [2026-09-13: Defer the shared composition-engine redesign and any extension-to-plugin rename. The prior Pursue verdict overclaimed: constructing a normalized Plite descriptor proves a lowering boundary, not a duplicate plugin engine. Plate has an independent product authoring/compiler job that should not move into Plite merely to erase an adapter noun. The credible cut is narrower: retain Plate compilation while prototyping one public nominal identity and one installed portal, deleting synthetic extension references, alias/topology remapping and late cross-contract dispatch. If that prototype cannot remove the machinery without a replacement map, public compiler graph or type-cost regression, keep layer-specific plugin/extension vocabulary over the shared installed owner.](review-records/2026-09-13-shared-plugin-identity-feasibility.json) — review; defer; defers. Bounded source and assertion inspection only. The review did not build the proposed identity model, run tests, measure TypeScript declaration/type-instantiation cost, replay publication/rollback, or benchmark runtime construction/configuration. The next investigation must implement a disposable raw Plite capability, configured headless Plate plugin and React Plate plugin on one public identity; preserve inference, dependency/conflict semantics, configuration precedence, schema behavior, independent editor stores, failed-candidate rollback, exact-view behavior and static/SSR isolation; and show an actual deletion inventory. A new compiled-descriptor family, metadata bag, alias registry or public compiler graph that merely replaces the old mapping fails the experiment. Current adoption/proof fields still describe the implemented September 12 contract, not this candidate.

- [2026-09-13: Pursue one shared plugin descriptor identity and plugin vocabulary while retaining Plate's product compiler. The matched prototype removed synthetic runtime descriptors, aliases, name remaps and portal-kind branches; preserved raw/headless/React lifetimes and rollback; and passed the frozen runtime and TypeScript budgets. The type contract must use a lazy owner-supplied capability provider. Raw Plite author fields remain available while installed behavior moves to a separate record.](review-records/2026-09-13-shared-plugin-identity-prototype.json) — review; pursue; reverses. The disposable model is architectural evidence, not production adoption proof. It ran ten correctness laws, 40 warm samples after eight warmups for 1/32/128/512 descriptors, one descriptive cold observation per cohort, and three interleaved TypeScript 7 diagnostics for 64 raw plus 64 Plate descriptors. Candidate runtime p50 stayed within 1.25x and TypeScript instantiations/memory/check time stayed within their frozen budgets. It did not run current package source, declaration emit, full editor mount/editing latency, browser behavior, packed releases or external consumers. Production implementation must replay those gates and may reverse to the honest split if it fails.

- [2026-09-13: Pursue is implemented and production-proven. Plite and Plate share one nominal immutable plugin descriptor identity; Plite installation owns separate normalized records, and Plate retains only its product compiler plus a private plitejs/internal lowering bridge. Public authoring and lookup use definePlugin, plugins and editor.plugin. Synthetic descriptors, alias/name topology translation and portal dispatch adapters are deleted. The final source-built benchmark beats or matches every frozen runtime/type budget, and package, declaration, SSR, DCE, browser, docs and migration proof passes.](review-records/2026-09-13-shared-plugin-identity-production.json) — review; pursue; reaffirms. Production proof covers 40 warm samples after eight warmups for each 1/32/128/512 cohort, three TypeScript runs over 64 raw plus 64 Plate descriptors, all Plite and Plate package partitions, packed NodeNext/Bundler/Node/SSR/DCE consumers and one Chromium exact mounted-view test. It does not claim a cross-browser matrix, published registry release or external application migration. A broader CLI aggregate encountered an existing watcher timeout cascade after 57 passing tests; the seven migration-focused CLI tests and package/release boundaries pass.

- [2026-09-14: Pursue declared construction inputs through configure, with extend retaining authoring/composition. The strongest cut is the public Plate/copied Yjs forwarding factories and handwritten native capability mirror. Current configure returning ConfiguredPlugin<C> is a revisable type contract, not proof that required host resources or conditional APIs require public factories. A generic declaration/adapter owner should preserve the factory input-to-result relation, specialize only declared capabilities, and create one binding lineage. This supersedes the blanket claim that non-widening configure forces these factories, while reaffirming shared descriptor identity and completed collaboration lifetime ownership.](review-records/2026-09-14-plugin-construction-inputs.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. Source-backed value verdict only. Existing production proof supports the unchanged baseline, not the proposed construction mechanism. No proposed call site was compiled, no product code changed, and no new runtime/performance/browser proof was run. Task design plan must prove the input/result type relation through raw/Plate/copied composition, required-input installation and typed author callbacks before binding, absent capabilities and validators, contextual/terminal override semantics, exact configured ancestry, independent bindings, rollback/cleanup and packed declarations. Any changed construction/runtime/type machinery remains provisional until its matched comparison passes. Adoption must repair current blanket non-widening and factory-only teaching through the doctrine owner.

- [2026-09-14: Stop the generic construction-input proposal and keep createYjsPlugin(options). The previous Pursue verdict was premature: YjsPlugin.configure(options) requires an unconfigured YjsPlugin that is either illegally installable without required resources or a new non-installable template state. That state would affect plugin tuples, dependencies, ancestry, terminal ordering, runtime validation and emitted types, while the source proves only one exported Plate feature constructor and one copied application wrapper. The factory performs a legitimate construction job by producing one option-specialized native binding, private token lineage, Plate lift and remote-selection contribution. The handwritten overload/type mirror is local debt to simplify only if possible without changing the public lifecycle.](review-records/2026-09-14-plugin-construction-inputs-reassessment.json) — review; stop; reverses. Source-backed Stop verdict. No product source changed and no behavior test, package build, declaration emission or performance benchmark was rerun because the selected target is the already implemented factory lifecycle. Existing shared-identity and collaboration records support that unchanged baseline only within their recorded limits. The review does not prove TypeScript cannot simplify the four local overloads; it finds that this local possibility does not justify a public plugin-template abstraction.

- [2026-09-14: Pursue a first-class mapped plugin-factory contract, while continuing to reject required construction inputs on configure or extend. Any options-to-plugin API is semantically a factory; hiding it behind YjsPlugin.configure does not remove construction and gives configure a second job. The stronger target keeps callable factories but adds a private input-to-exact-descriptor witness and result mapping, so native yjs can be adapted by Plate and then copied UI without Plate redeclaring API groups, four capability overloads or another generic forwarding function. Unlike an incomplete plugin template, factory mapping adds no install state or editor runtime owner and every call still returns one complete nominal descriptor.](review-records/2026-09-14-plugin-factory-mapping.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. Source-backed Pursue verdict, not an accepted signature or implementation. The throwaway compile establishes that naive inference is insufficient; it does not prove a particular higher-kinded encoding, mapping syntax, declaration shape or cost. No factory-mapping candidate was implemented, emitted, benchmarked or exercised at runtime. Design must compare a private witness plus standalone mapper against a callable factory with map, prove direct and copied validators and absent capabilities, preserve fresh descriptor/token identity and ancestry, and measure declaration size, TypeScript cost, DCE and runtime construction. Existing collaboration behavior remains proven only by its prior record.

- [2026-09-14: Pursue is implemented and adopted. platejs/yjs/react exports a concrete YjsPlugin factory whose create(options) preserves native conditional APIs and validator-derived cursor data, whose require(key).map(stage) derives copied factories through the existing Plate author-stage model, and whose factory object cannot be installed or configured. Created descriptors remain ordinary fresh descriptors with extend and terminal configure. The handwritten Plate and copied factories are deleted, callers and docs use create, focused package and copied UI proof passes, clean declarations are finite, DCE succeeds, and measured type/runtime costs stay within every frozen budget.](review-records/2026-09-14-plugin-factory-mapping-implementation.json) — review; pursue; reaffirms. Focused Plite Yjs, Plate Yjs/Yjs React/React-core, permanent type-contract, copied registry, table caller, architecture-tooling, clean entrypoint declaration, runtime build, DCE, registry and doctrine proof passes. The package-wide Plate typecheck and declaration build stop in the unrelated changed SuggestionPlugin.tsx on TS2883/TS4023/TS7056; the website typecheck consequently stops at the missing packed platejs/dist/index.d.ts API-reference prerequisite. The clean Yjs declaration partition was rebuilt after deleting stale incremental output and contains no old factory. The benchmark baseline is an exact reconstruction from the task's completed pre-change local source because that state was never committed. No browser matrix, package publication, release, commit or external application migration is claimed.

<a id="persistence"></a>
## Document serialization and migrations

What must a saved document retain, validate and migrate, and which durable-state or migration layers can be eliminated?

Review: unassessed. Adoption: not-assessed. Proof: not-replayed. Latest record / freshness: none / unknown.

Payoff 9/10: Storage contracts outlive mounted views and must remain coherent without requiring collaboration or generated application schemas.

Review prerequisites: [state](#state). The durable-state contract determines what serialization and migrations must preserve. Next owner: best-api.

Related questions/history (not prerequisites): [history](#history), [collaboration](#collaboration), [compiler](#compiler).

Consumers: [document-migration-demo.tsx](../../apps/www/src/registry/examples/document-migration-demo.tsx), [bin.ts](../../packages/cli/src/bin.ts).

Proof entry points (not replayed by this ledger): [value-codec.test.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/value-codec.test.ts), [run-migration.test.ts](../../packages/cli/test/run-migration.test.ts), [history-persistence-contract.spec.ts](../../packages/plitejs/test/history/history-persistence-contract.spec.ts).

## Suggestions and tracked-change review

Which proposed-edit, accept/reject and attribution policies belong to Suggestions, and which should reuse native authored changes?

Review: reviewed. Adoption: adopted. Proof: verified. Latest record / freshness: 2026-09-14-suggestions-view-initialization-api / stale.

Payoff 9/10: User review semantics and copied discussion UI must not create a second change-history authority.

Review prerequisites: [selection](#selection). Proposed edits and accept/reject behavior must obey committed-change and selection laws; authored integration is contextual. Next owner: task.

Inspection: 2026-09-14 adopted implementation: Plite authored remains the sole state and decision owner and publishes exact affected changes and node keys. Plate Base Suggestion owns the two-mode facade and neutral authored markers; Suggestion React owns mounted-view active state, hooks and targeted decoration refresh. Registry source owns cards, styling, toolbars and the optional Comments join. All in-repository consumers use the package entrypoints, and the former independent Plate suggestion mutation engine is deleted. The September 14 content-attributes correction keeps optional feature selectors out of generic Editor skins: copied SuggestionKit configures render.contentAttributes, compiled once by Plate and applied to the existing live/static root.

Gaps: Verified against package contracts, generated declarations and artifacts, registry tests and generation, a fresh 10-journey Chromium replay, and deterministic two-view performance cohorts through 50,000 nodes and 5,000 changes. Claim limits: local Chromium and deterministic fixtures only; native/mobile/assistive behavior, production RUM, multi-author network transport and the independent broad Google behavior audit were not assessed. This correction adds 155 focused core tests, four source/type-contract gates, 17 registry tests, an 11-journey Chromium replay and six bounded construction/SSR cost cohorts. Full www TypeScript remains stopped at an independently reproduced Footnote TS2769 error; no broader parity or browser-performance claim is added.

Plans/context: [2026-09-13-suggestion-package-over-authored-changes.md](../../docs/plans/2026-09-13-suggestion-package-over-authored-changes.md)

Current compiled decision: [suggestion-review-semantics.md](../../docs/research/decisions/suggestion-review-semantics.md).

History:

- [2026-09-13: Pursue complete suggestion semantics through native per-change details and Plate review. Cut kind-plus-text-ranges as the sole review contract, the first-200-pending card assumption, and silent decision refusals. Keep native authored identity and causal decisions; restoring main would restore duplicate mutation logic and known structural/property limits. Actual Enter/Backspace and valued-mark review lifecycles are the first bounded design question.](review-records/2026-09-13-suggestions-google-docs-audit.json) — review; pursue; initial. 32 families inventoried and reviewed, none excluded. Google evidence: 19 families with a live subset, seven documentation-only, six unresolved; no complete parity certificate. Twenty-five probe rows include repeated actions and selected decisions on a synthetic tab using one author in Chrome/macOS. No second-author Google session, offline replay, real IME, mobile, DOCX roundtrip, full clipboard/move lifecycle or Google benchmark. Current and historical tests were inspected, not rerun. The main manifest counts 19 test files and 101 literal test cases; current WWW has five suggestion tests and Plite authored browser has 39 declarations with parameterization. Model-delta coverage does not imply actual feature-command or UI coverage. No product code, API export, benchmark trial or release changed. Pursue accepts further coupled design work, not a measured new runtime or universal superiority.

- [2026-09-13: Supersede the first audit's 32-family completeness boundary with 44 selected families: 37 Google-facing and seven Plate/Plite platform/lifecycle families. Reaffirm one native authored owner and the cut of kind-plus-ranges as the sole review contract. The target must also cover producer group cardinality and outcomes, author withdrawal, durable closed review provenance, global scopes, live paging recovery, and explicit AI/external-text/anchor/retention/codec/lifetime laws.](review-records/2026-09-13-suggestions-omission-audit.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. Forty-four selected families were reviewed: 37 Google-facing and seven Plate/Plite platform/lifecycle. Google evidence counts are 19 live-subset, 12 documentation-only, and six unresolved; no complete parity certificate. The machine crosswalk validates 48 unique current Google request union members, including eight explicitly unsupported suggest-mode requests, and 32 unique F-contracts against the 44 audit rows. The original 25 live probes remain one-author, online Chrome/macOS evidence. No new Google document mutation, second-author session, offline replay, real IME, native mobile, assistive-technology run, full format roundtrip, matched Google benchmark, product edit, test suite, or fourth performance trial occurred. The crosswalk proves inventory within its named sources, not implementation or UI parity.

- [2026-09-13: Pursue a Plate suggestion feature with reusable semantic review and React integration over the canonical authored owner. Remove copied cast/subscription/decoration protocols where their existing owner can own them. Keep themes, cards, toolbar layout, kit membership and comments composition copyable. Do not restore a second suggestion engine or rename all authored APIs to suggestion.](review-records/2026-09-13-suggestions-entrypoint-review.json) — review; pursue; reaffirms. Single-proposal source review, not a repeat of the 44-family behavior audit. No product edit, test replay, browser session, native-device proof, benchmark or package build. Source inspection supports ownership and existing call-path claims only. The selected runtime remains provisional pending exact-view lifetime, projection-only invalidation, comments independence, inferred public calls, package/peer closure and complete-operation scale proof. Existing tests were inspected only. Public exports are proposed; no new signature is certified.

- [2026-09-14: Pursue plugin-owned render.contentAttributes and remove optional-feature knowledge from generic Editor skins.](review-records/2026-09-14-suggestions-content-attributes.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. 155 focused core tests, 17 registry tests, root/React-core/static source typechecks, public type contracts, targeted lint, registry generation and all 11 local Chromium journeys pass. The six 1/10/100-plugin by 1/1000-node prototype and production cohorts pass a fixed max(1ms,10%) p50 overhead budget; SSR/construction on a noisy shared host is a bounded cost guard, not a speedup or browser/native performance claim. Zero additional scans/subscriptions are source-inferred. Full www TypeScript stops at Footnote TS2769 line 389; an in-memory compiler-host control removing both new BasePlugin declarations reproduces the identical diagnostic. Native/mobile/assistive behavior, remote collaboration and complete Google Docs parity were not assessed.

- [2026-09-14: Pursue deleting mounted-editor fixture replay and actor switching: hydrate the authored document and matching comments directly, and reuse native mounted-view initialization instead of a mode effect.](review-records/2026-09-14-suggestions-initialization.json) — review; pursue; reaffirms. Source-only, bounded initialization/API ownership review. No product changes, browser replay, runtime benchmark or parity certification. Existing save/reload and view inputs were inspected, not rerun. The proposed facade must prove stable identities and linked targets, empty initial local undo history, first-render projection, Strict Mode/remount behavior and two independent views. Scope-wide fingerprints are provenance, not a claim that every suggestion operation was audited.

- [2026-09-14: Stop moving initialization into SuggestionPlugin configuration. Keep EditorRoot authored: it is the only compared contract that initializes the exact native view before render without global plugin state, hidden precedence, late effects or another wrapper vocabulary.](review-records/2026-09-14-suggestions-view-initialization-api.json) — review; stop; reaffirms. This is a bounded API and ownership reassessment, not a new runtime execution. Matching implementation evidence records 20 Chromium suggestion journeys, focused view-lifetime contracts, full www typechecking and installed editor builds. Those receipts prove the implemented behavior, not performance or complete Google Docs parity. The full Plate type-contract suite still has the separately recorded table TS2589 limitation.

History:

- [2026-09-12: Pursue neutral source-owned public names and one data-editor DOM contract; delete unnecessary markers and avoid mechanical Editor prefixes or per-layer aliases. Resolve React/provider/plugin collisions and treat wire names as data migrations.](review-records/2026-09-12-editor-public-naming.json) — review; pursue; initial. Current-source review and a bounded independent DOM source review only. Existing focused tests were located, not run. No browser, packed-export, inference, performance or wire-migration pass is claimed. Naming reuses the existing runtime; replacement lookup machinery is not accepted. Baseline global review-ledger check already fails on unrelated stale registry/blocks inventory; the new record is separately validated and source-fingerprinted.

- [2026-09-12: Pursue EditorRoot as the public root component in each React entrypoint, retain the independently useful passive EditorProvider, and adopt data-editor-authored through coordinated codecs.](review-records/2026-09-12-editor-public-naming-components.json) — review; pursue; reaffirms. Source inspection and prior matching review evidence only; no implementation, package tests, browser proof or authored format migration was run. Root naming changes no runtime machinery. Exact other export names are not covered. Global review inventory remains independently stale.

- [2026-09-12: Keep EditorRoot and EditorContent. Stop the Editable-prefix rename: there is no identifier collision with Editor, and the family includes composition and read-only presentation.](review-records/2026-09-12-editor-public-naming-editable-comparison.json) — review; stop; reaffirms. Source and current official documentation review only. The Root/Content pattern is established, not a universal editor standard; Base UI uses Popup for Dialog. No behavior, browser, performance or new implementation claim. The global review inventory was already stale during prior closure.

- [2026-09-13: Pursue the remaining hard cut. Top-level exports, DOM attributes and ordinary Plate docs are clean, but one public proof field, five browser protocol keys, ten public JSDoc declarations and Plate declaration provenance still expose Plite.](review-records/2026-09-13-editor-public-naming-closure-audit.json) — review; pursue; supersedes. Exhaustive source and emitted declaration inspection for current package export maps plus exact content/docs search. Counts are 67 source entrypoints/2,959 export occurrences, 68 emitted entrypoints/2,960 occurrences, and 3,026 resolved public declarations. No product code changed. Runtime, packed-package installation, browser behavior, native-device behavior and external-consumer tooltips were not replayed; generated dist is current checkout evidence rather than a newly packed release.
